ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u18	We proceed with the examination of item 1 of the agenda: The Extraordinary Commission's report on the government draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of innovative activities (prints Nos 1934 and 1981). ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u19	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on Deregulation, I would like to present a report on the Government's draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of innovative activities, contained in print No 1934. The first reading of this draft law took place at the 50th meeting of the Sejm on 27 October this year. The project was examined by the Extraordinary Commission on deregulation on 7 November, which welcomed both its main objectives and the detailed arrangements for their implementation. The bill is cross-cutting and comprehensive. This means that although it includes changes in several different laws, they all have one common denominator. This is, of course, about the improvement of the legal environment mentioned in the title of the Act, and therefore the removal of barriers and the building of system support for innovative activities. Using this specificity of the draft law, the deregulation committee outside the editorial and legislative amendment group introduced several substantive amendments. The Commission has unanimously delivered a positive opinion on the draft law. This project has a positive opinion of the Social Dialogue Council, which brings together more than 100 associations. This is another bill prepared by Minister Jarosław Gowin, which was created in consensus with all political communities. In the opinion of the committee, the draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of innovative activities should be assessed positively. It is an important step to strengthen cooperation between science and business in Poland, which will allow us to build an innovative economy. In view of the above, on behalf of the Extraordinary Commission for Deregulation, I would ask the High Sejm to approve the attached bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u20	Thank you, sir. I would like to inform you that the Sejm has agreed that we will hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles in the discussion on this item. I'm opening a discussion. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u21	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The need to improve the regulatory environment for innovative activities was raised in the exposé by Ms Beata Szydło, President of the Council of Ministers, delivered on 18 November 2015. During the work of the Council for Innovation and Interministerial Innovation Team, established on 18 January 2016, the concept of rapid implementation into Polish law of the best described instruments for innovation support was created, as reflected in the Act of 4 November 2016 amending certain laws defining the conditions for conducting innovative activities. At the same time, work has been undertaken on the preparation of a draft second law on innovation. The drafting of this draft law responds to the demands of different environments, in particular entrepreneurs, scientific entities and investors. Prime Minister Jarosław Gowin, Minister of Science and Higher Education, conducted a very broad public consultation even before he started writing the project. This allowed for precise identification of shortcomings in the system of law restricting Polish innovation. The final conclusions of the consultation are set out in the Innovation White Paper. The bill itself was passed to more than 80 entities and was positively assessed by the Joint Committee of the Government and the Territorial Government. The revised after the first stage of the consultation reached almost 60 actors. This stage was very carefully prepared. The bill is horizontal in nature, i.e. implies the amendment of a number of laws affecting the conditions for innovation activities. Each of these changes is slightly different in nature, but in order to identify the main thematic blocks, it is important to mention, first, the tax changes, the most important of which is to increase the amount of the R &amp; D reduction to 100%. costs incurred, regardless of the size of the trader. The second thematic block is to create opportunities for a wider involvement of pro-innovative public institutions. In particular, it is about enabling the Minister to announce programmes in the area of innovation of the economy, as well as introducing new forms of action of the National Centre for Research and Development and the Polish Agency for Enterprise Development in the form of e.g. financial instruments. The third thematic block includes science and higher education. In this area, it should be noted that scientific units in the form of companies should be extended, the scope of monitoring of the fate of graduates should be extended to include PhD students and the deregulation of the procedure for determining the Polish road map of research infrastructure. The Extraordinary Commission on deregulation, which I have the honour to chair, has introduced a number of clarification and drafting amendments to the proposal, as well as substantive amendments that raise its pro-innovation value. Thanks to the amendments, among others. We will facilitate the financing of start-ups or expand the possibility of carrying out the research necessary for digitization progress. At the same time, I would like to emphasise how important it is to continue the smooth process of the project. In view of the fact that it covers tax arrangements, it should be announced within a time limit allowing taxable persons to familiarise themselves with it and to apply it from 1 January 2018. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Poland's development model in recent years, even though it allowed economic growth to continue, was not a direct result of innovation. To get out of the so-called. medium development traps, it is necessary to exploit the creative potential of Polish entrepreneurs and scientists and build a knowledge-based economy. In my opinion, today's bill is an important step towards achieving this objective. It contains a number of deregulation solutions and aims to strengthen the public support system for innovative entrepreneurs. With this in mind, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of innovative activities. Moreover, we are convinced that this project can be adopted unanimously by the High House. To the draft Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will make another amendment, which is the result of the consensual and constructive work of the deregulation committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[What's that?]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Joachim Brudziński]]

And here I would like to draw your attention to some distinctions, differences that exist in two types of companies. The provisions which have been put forward on the matter concern special purpose vehicles. The purpose companies are not the same as the so-called ones. spin-off companies, i.e. companies that are used to commercialise research. The main difference is that 100 percent of the target company is owned by a university - and in this sense it is to some extent public - whereas companies that are called to commercialise research have very often already external shareholders who enter with their capital, and in this sense they are no longer public companies and they can act as any other company. Our limitations on ownership apply only to special purpose vehicles, because they are equipped with public property. Therefore, if they are equipped with public property, they should be subject to the same rules as those of all entities that have public property. On the other hand, special purpose companies can set up spin-offs, i.e. they can set up companies dedicated to commercialization, specific research and then operate in an absolutely identical way, as flexibly as all others. But besides the need to tell the truth about the tragic events of the past, there is a duty to appreciate those who save Poles and pay for it often with their lives and their families. We also want to commemorate Poles saving their fellow citizens. It is important to appreciate those who witnessed to the truth about the crimes in the Polish nation or helped to come to it, often exposing themselves to repression. We have an unpaid debt of gratitude to these people. We want to pay our respects. The task of the Republic of Poland is therefore to express gratitude to those righteous who are still alive and to consolidate their heroism. They are the greatest friends of Poland and Poles who, in the most wonderful way possible, gave a witness with personal heroism and dedication in the hour of trial. Tens of years after tragic events most heroes, whose Polish collectiveity owes gratitude, are already dead. In many cases, the Polish state will therefore be able to honor the representatives of the next generations. We must also remember those who often in remote parts of the world gave refuge to refugees from inhumane lands. Without them, many Polish lives would not survive. Appreciation of noble people and deeds, expressing gratitude to the righteous is an action in international relations that brings out and exposes what is best and most honorable in relations between nations. Thus, such activities can be the greatest steps leading to mutual understanding and reconciliation. The Institute's tasks, in accordance with the provisions of the Act, include: conducting and supporting scientific research, conducting and expanding databases, books and archives, popularising this issue through publishing activities, production of audiovisual materials and other activities. The bodies of the Institute shall be the Director and the Council of Remembrance. There must be an institution which not only documents, as already existing, what crimes were committed on Poles and citizens of the Republic, but also must document the memory of those who saved or helped to find out the truth about the crimes which were committed on Poles and citizens of the Republic. The most important thing for us is that we can tell the world out loud: there are people - our neighbors, but also the citizens of the world - who gave their lives for Poles, gave their lives for their neighbours, gave their lives for citizens of the Republic. We really deserve to know this story and tell it. We also want to honor those people, those who are still alive. When time passes naturally, we also want their descendants to know what their ancestors had. We want to be remembered not only for those who saved lives, risking their lives, but also for those who, like the people of the Russian Memorial, helped us to come to the truth, e.g. About the crimes in Katyn. We want to celebrate and appreciate those who saved Polish existence, not necessarily putting their lives at risk, but by sacrificing their efforts, money, commitment, for example. Save Polish orphans, just like the Prime Minister New Zealand. More than 700 Polish children orphaned in Siberia, who left with Anders' army, found shelter there. New Zealand was one of the few countries in the world that responded to the London Government's appeal for shelter for Polish orphans. We also want to appreciate India. Polish children also survived there. It's not just gonna be a story about suffering and Martyrology. It will also be a story about how great, how noble were those who engaged in saving Poland, the Republic of Poland, Polish citizens. I want to make a few amendments, which first of all clean up and clarify the functioning of this institute. We want to introduce e.g. the term of office of the director, i.e. these are organisational amendments, but two of them are very important. We're proposing a change of name. The applicants proposed a name: the Institute of Solidarity and Courage. We want to name the Institute of Solidarity and Fortitude. It seems that courage in relation to the actions of these people we want to talk about is a phrase behind a narrow one. It was bravery, it required bravery sometimes unimaginable, so we would like to change the name. The second very important amendment is the introduction of a new award. We want these people to be decorated with the Virtus et Fraternitas medal, which is the medal of bravery and brotherhood. This special award will be given by the President of the Republic at the request of the Institute. Of course, the Institute will also retain the right of initiative in the field of broadcasting through notification [[♪ The bell ♪]] to the Ministry of the Eastern Cross and the Western Cross, as well as other important Polish decorations. Thank you very much and I ask you to support this project. It seems obvious that such an institute should work for a long time. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u87	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on Changes in Codifications, I have the honour to present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters and some other laws. Ladies and gentlemen! I will briefly point out that this is a project that technically solves the problem of spending funds at the stage of the criminal investigation in situations where the investigation is conducted by an authority which is not subject to the State Treasury unit, and most often concerns a local government unit such as the municipal guard, such as city service, local and local government. At present, the rules do not allow the payment of advances under this procedure relating to expenditure on experts, witnesses, experts and undoubtedly there was a legal gap, namely that, even in these proceedings, the Treasury was ultimately obliged to spend funds on these advances, which also involved an extension of the procedure, because it most often remained entirely within the jurisdiction of the local government. Hence the proposal from the Ministry to settle this legal gap. The Extraordinary Commission for Codification Changes after considering this project on 12 October 2017 requests the High Sejm to adopt the Act in the text of the draft annexed to the report. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u89	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u93	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am pleased to present the position of the club in the scope of the government draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters and some other laws. The aim of the project is to address the problem of covering the costs of criminal proceedings in the course of investigations carried out by authorities under the authority of the local government, in general municipal and municipal guards. As I said at the first reading of the project, there is no doubt that there was a legal gap here. The current legal situation leads to an unacceptable situation in which the same citizen, depending on which investigative authority he will have to present himself as a witness, may be reimbursed once, otherwise not. Eliminating interpretation doubts about expenditure incurred in the course of investigations, such as the reimbursement of witness travel expenses or lost earnings, is very important in terms of the effectiveness of pending proceedings. Our doubts - and this was also in the codification committee - are raised by the article. 4 of this law, according to which the municipality applies to the court for a declaration of enforceability and obtains an enforceable title, i.e. immediately passes to enforcement proceedings, which is described in the Code of Civil Procedure. It should therefore be considered whether the proposed indirect element should be introduced into the proposed project, which will enable the municipality to apply for reimbursement in advance to the person responsible. It was also raised by the Legislative Office. However, despite these reservations, Members of the House and Modern Members will vote in favour of this government submission. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u95	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters and certain other laws. In the draft submitted, the applicant sought to address the problem of covering the costs of criminal proceedings in the course of investigations carried out by an authority under the authority of a local authority. We are talking here, above all, about explanatory activities carried out by municipal guards or city guards. It's about getting the witness's due back. Those expenditure should be temporarily covered by the municipalities to which the municipal or municipal guards are subject as their organisational unit under Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 law on municipal guards. However, there is no legal basis for imposing such an obligation on municipalities under the present law. Similarly, it concerns the question of identifying the entity responsible for the reimbursement of costs incurred in the investigation of offences carried out by the municipal or municipal guards. Here, too, is a legal gap that the applicant rightly wants to remove. The proposed amendment to the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters therefore applies in principle only to Section XIII, i.e. costs of proceedings. However, this is a very important change because the responsible state authorities must ensure that the cost of civil, administrative, criminal proceedings or, as in this case, criminal proceedings is regulated in a precise and non-excessive way for the parties, including the Treasury. Several amendments of a substantive and editorial nature were adopted during the committee's work. Among other things, it is rightly pointed out that the proposed wording of the Article. 119 § 1 of the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters should be referred not to the convicted, but to the convicted, since such a term is used by the legislator in this law, as in the Code of Offences. It is also worth pointing out the point that one of the representatives of the Legislative Office present at the committee meeting pointed out. We're talking about art here. 4 the law implementing Article. 206 Law of the Criminal Code implementing § 4. It excludes the application of Article to local self-government units. 206 § 1 stating that the court invites the person liable to pay the court's costs within 30 days and, in the event of an unsuccessful expiry of the time limit, they shall be collected by execution. As a result of such a construction, the local government unit will not have a legal basis to call on a person who is obliged to pay costs on an amicable basis and will have to apply immediately for a declaration of enforceability under the enforcement title. The chairman of the committee, Mr Marek Ast, proposed that the question of considering the validity of the amendment in this regard should be resolved in the course of further work on the bill. In this respect, I believe that the amendment by which a self-government unit, before it starts the execution of judicial costs, will call on the person obliged to pay them voluntarily within a specified period, is, of course, justified and justified. Adoption of a proposal which, as a rule, obliges the local government unit to direct the case immediately to enforcement without the possibility of acting on an amicable path, will expose the obliged person, ready to settle the debt immediately, to bear additional, completely unnecessary costs associated with the execution of the bailiff. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle agrees in this respect with the position expressed by the representatives of the Legislative Office, with the approval of the representative of the Legislative Department of the Ministry of Justice, requesting that the wording of the following Article be amended accordingly. 206 § 4 of the Implementing Penal Code. The Free and Solidarni Circle will support the proposed bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u99	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Extraordinary Committee on Justice. changes in the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Offences, Prints Nos 1814 and 1883. The draft concerns the introduction of rules which clearly indicate the entity responsible for the reimbursement of costs incurred in the course of criminal investigations carried out by an authority under the authority of a local government unit. In the current state of law, there is no legal basis for imposing a temporary obligation on the municipality to pay expenditure incurred in the course of investigations carried out by the municipal or municipal guards. The project therefore provides for the following solutions: 1) expenditure incurred in the course of investigations carried out by an authority under the authority of a local government unit will be temporarily provided by the competent local authority unit and will then be settled, as has been the case so far, by the State Treasury, in accordance with the rules of liability for the outcome of proceedings; 2. expenditure to be provided by the State Treasury and the local government unit will be flat-rate - a lump sum would not include the claims of experts or institutions appointed for the opinion and the non-payment by the parties of aid granted ex officio by lawyers or legal advisers; 3. the Minister for Justice shall determine in the Regulation the amount of flat-rate expenditure in criminal proceedings in the course of investigations and in the course of judicial proceedings; 4. flat-rate expenditure and other charges to the Treasury or local government units shall be ordered from the sentenced person; 5. the costs of the proceedings will be borne by the Treasury or by the local government, respectively, in the event of the termination of proceedings or the acquittal of the blamed person. During the committee's work, amendments were made mainly editorial and legislative. In this situation, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the bill submitted in the draft.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u100	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters and some other laws (prints 1814 and 1883). The aim of the project is to address the problem of covering the costs of criminal proceedings in the course of investigations carried out by an authority under the authority of a local government unit. Expenditure incurred in the course of investigations is to be regulated by the proposed regulation temporarily by the State Treasury or the local government unit, and subsequently settled by application to liability for the outcome of proceedings. The proposal introduces a rule that, if the investigation of offences is carried out by a municipal guard or a municipal guard, the reimbursement of expenditure will take place from the municipality's budget. If these activities are carried out by the police or other public service, the reimbursement of witness expenses will come from the state budget. The expenditure will return accordingly to the budget of the municipality or the Treasury after the court has completed its proceedings. They will be charged with a lump sum by a convicted felon. It was therefore assumed that the expenditure incurred by the State Treasury or local government unit would be flat-rate in order to settle the expenditure in the final decision - the flat-rate will not cover the costs of appointment of experts and the costs of unpaid legal assistance provided by lawyers or legal advisers. Until now, only the costs of legal proceedings have been borne by the convicted person. Only in the event of termination of proceedings or of acquittal of the accused will the costs of proceedings be borne by the State Treasury or by the local government unit. Thus, in the event of the withdrawal of proceedings or the acquittal of the accused, the costs of the proceedings will be borne by the State Treasury or by the local government unit. On the other hand, flat-rate expenses and other claims to the Treasury or to the local government unit will be ordered from the sentenced person. The amount of flat-rate expenditure in criminal proceedings in the course of investigations and in the course of judicial proceedings is to be determined by the Minister of Justice in the relevant regulation. High Izbo! On the basis of the current rules, it is difficult to clearly identify who is required to regulate the type of expenditure concerned: the Treasury or e.g. the municipal guard being an organisational unit of the municipality. The municipal (urban) guard is an organisational unit of the municipality, while the municipality has legal personality and therefore its budget is separate from the State Treasury budget. Currently, interpretation doubts cause that the witness's right, expert's right, interpreter's right, specialist's right to pay the fees granted to them for their involvement in criminal investigations carried out by the municipal guard is not exercised. The solution to this problem is necessary in order to ensure proper enforcement of the witness, expert, translator, specialist for the payment of the fees granted to them for their involvement in criminal investigations carried out by the municipal (urban) guard. The proposed regulation will eliminate the ambiguity of interpretation and ensure that witnesses receive the right remuneration also when a local authority is a party to the case. It seems appropriate for such institutions to participate in the costs of judicial proceedings in local self-government units. From now on, a clear division will be introduced: if the investigation is carried out by the police, the reimbursement of expenditure will be made from the state budget, and if the investigation is carried out by the municipal guard or city guard, the reimbursement of expenditure will be carried out from the municipal budget.

The result is that we have very little shooting range in Poland. Many old shooters, especially hunters, built by hunting wheels with their own funds, were closed because they could not meet the new strict technical and safety requirements. The result is that even the appropriate service equipped with firearms and having an obligation to systematically train itself in its use often has a problem finding shooters where they can improve their skills. In recent years, around 60 military shooters have been liquidated under the coalition of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party in Poland. There are only 52 garrison shooters left. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u190	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The draft law amending certain laws to improve the consistency of the terminology of the legal system concerns four legal acts, namely the Law of 26 June 1974. The Labour Code, secondly, the Law of 18 April 1985 on the recognition by the courts of labour law and social security cases, thirdly, the Law of 24 June 1999 on the implementation of the legislative initiative by citizens and finally the Act of 5 January 2011. The bill is a reaction of the Committee on Petitions of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland to a petition addressed to the Sejm by Mr Sebastian Adamowicz, which the committee considered justified. The purpose of the Act is to make minor changes to the five laws adapting the terminology in them to the current legal situation. The introduction of these amendments will make it easier for the addressees to revise the legislation, to understand it and to apply it properly in the current legal environment. As far as the Labour Code is concerned, there is a reference to voivodship courts that no longer exist in the Polish judicial system, and it is therefore proposed to make a comprehensive amendment to the article. 262 taking into account both the replacement of regional courts with district courts and the restoration of appeals courts. It also appears necessary to amend the Law of 18 April 1985 on the recognition of labour and social security cases by the courts. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Sorry, this one's about Traffic Law. It's been crossed out. But there's another amendment to this bill, which is the Election Code Act of January 5th. There is the concept of a health care establishment that has been eliminated from legal trading by the Law of 15 April 2011 on medicinal activities. This law replaced the concept of "health care establishment' with the term "health operator'. It is worth noting that the essential socio-legal effect of the proposed law will be to improve the coherence and transparency of the legal system. Due to its nature and limited scope, the law will not have economic effects as well as financial implications for the public finance sector. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, with this amendment that I had the opportunity to pass on to your marshal, intends to support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by 3: The editor-in-chief of the journal or journal must not be a person convicted of crimes against the fundamental political and economic interests of the Polish People's Republic. Similar flowers, you can say, we can find e.g. in the Act on Socio-Professional Organisations of Farmers, for example in the provision that individual farms are a sustainable and equal element of the socio-economic system of the Polish People's Republic. In our assessment of the action to organise and ensure consistency of the legal system of the Republic of Poland should go as far as possible during this term of the Sejm and be reduced to a comprehensive review of the most important laws concerning particular branches of the law in order to eliminate all kinds of contradictions, old terminology or outdated references to legal acts. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u210	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Law and Justice Club regarding the government bill on insurance distribution. According to the Directive, customers should benefit from protection at the same level, regardless of the differences between distribution channels. It is therefore essential that insurance distributors operate in a level playing field. The draft law assumes that insurance intermediaries should be classified as brokers and insurance agents, subject to the inadmissibility of the merger of both functions. Such a solution is justified in the current market practice and the reasonableness of such a solution. An entity's breakdown is a classic picture of insurance mediation where only brokers or agents registered as brokers can perform mediation activities. This division also applies to persons engaged in brokerage activities. Consequently, it is proposed to maintain division into agency activities and agency activities carried out by insurance agents and brokerage activities and brokerage activities carried out by insurance brokers. In addition, it is proposed that agency activities and agency activities be carried out in addition to insurance agents also by agents offering supplementary insurance. The draft law provides for the direct performance by the insurance undertaking of the activities relating to the distribution of insurance by the employees' insurance undertaking. Such activities are defined as the distribution activities of the insurance undertaking. Similarly, it is proposed that reinsurance undertakings entitled to carry out reinsurance distribution activities shall perform the reinsurance undertaking's distribution activities. Until now, insurance intermediation has also included reinsurance intermediation. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed law proposes that insurance distribution also means carrying out preparatory activities to conclude, conclude and assist in the administration and performance of insurance guarantee contracts, as well as in matters of compensation or benefit. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u214	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The Act lays down rules on the exercise of activities in the field of distribution of personal and property insurance and the distribution of reinsurance. The bill imposes on brokers and insurance agents a number of new information obligations on customers. They shall be required to present them before the contract is concluded. They should inform the client in a fair and consistent manner about the principles of complaint and out-of-court dispute settlement and, which is quite controversial, must provide information on the nature of the remuneration received in connection with the contract offered. The insurance distributor will also be required to determine the customer's requirements and needs on the basis of information obtained from the customer and to provide him with objective information about the insurance contract in order to enable him to make an informed decision. In the course of its work, the Public Finance Commission amended the definition of the client in the case of insurance guarantee contracts and identified it as the payer of the insurance guarantee and introduced the definition of the seeker for insurance protection. Paragraph 16 has also been added. 6, which concerns the provision of information on complaints by insurance agents and supplementary insurance agents who perform agency activities for more than one insurance undertaking under the same heading. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 20 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 14 was divided into three successive paragraphs, which fosters a better understanding of the provisions of the Act. In Chapter 2, Article 2. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 concerning the provision by the insurance undertaking of information before the conclusion of the life insurance contract, the need to provide information on the distribution cost index and the cost index of the commission of the person performing the distribution activity of the insurance undertaking has been changed to the need to provide information on the amount of the distribution cost index related to the proposed contract. Modern is in favour of this bill. It is intended to implement EU law, but it also covers the implementation of other legislation and is therefore a comprehensive regulation. What is very important is that it strengthens the protection of people who have insurance contracts, which are, after all, an important part of our lives. Customers have the right to be properly informed about the provisions of the insurance contract before it is signed, so that they are fully aware of the decision as to whether or not to sign such a contract. That is why we are in favour of this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

These directives introduce a single legal regime for the insurance market, which is very important, in all the Member States of the European Union and the countries of the European Economic Area, and leave in principle a small margin to regulate the law in this respect by individual Member States. This is why the bill allows you to provide cross-border insurance services. The Act obliges very widely understood information activities in relation to clients, supervisors, i.e. the Commission for Financial Supervision, but also the European Insurance and Occupational Pensions Authority. The draft standardizes the rules for the production, approval and sale of products, as well as the keeping of certain records concerning products and entities in the insurance field. [[♪ The bell ♪]] In general, it should be stressed that the draft law, through the included information, organisational and supervisory requirements, is a pro-consumer project that raises the awareness and safety of insured persons. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of this project. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u228	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance a report on the Government's draft law amending the law on value added tax and certain other laws. At its 49th meeting, on 11 October, the Sejm sent the above draft to the Committee on Public Finances for consideration. The Commission examined the draft on 26 October 2017 and asks the High House to approve the attached draft law. The main objective of the bill is to fight against dishonesty, fight against VAT carousels, fight against the VAT gap, i.e. to seal the VAT system in Poland. The second objective is to improve the stability of VAT receipts. I think that a very important goal is also to give Polish entrepreneurs tools so that they can effectively protect themselves against unwanted participation in VAT carousels and all the consequences. The Act introduces an innovative, albeit based on the experiences of several other countries of the European Union, solution from English called split payment, or split payment mechanism. What is this mechanism? Payments resulting from VAT invoices to be made by the taxable person to his suppliers, his service providers, will be distributed into two sources: one payment will be made in a net amount into the normal account of the trader, the other payment, the amount of VAT resulting from the invoice, will be made into a separate VAT account, and from this separate VAT account the trader will be able to transfer VAT to another invoice, somewhere to his supplier, or to transfer this obligation, VAT, to the tax office. This bill will be safe, you can't steal money from this account, which is actually the end of the VAT carousel. If entrepreneurs use this tool, it will not be possible to use the mechanism of a typical carousel. Of course, it is voluntary to use this solution, but the law proposes incentives to use this solution. If the trader and his contractor choose to benefit from a split payment, if this VAT is paid on a separate VAT account, they will be exempt from the so-called "tax." joint responsibility for the dishonesty of its counterparty. In turn, for those who accept such payment, it will certainly be an incentive that any refund of VAT will be received on VAT account in an accelerated period. During the committee meeting, I think all our political groups agreed that this solution was valuable, that it would secure our tax system, that the government had simply done a good job in preparing this bill. Some of the details probably did not appeal to some of you, although it is worth noting that the opposition Members did not make any amendments and thus did not submit any minority proposals to this draft law, while some amendments were tabled by Mr Sylvester Tułaev of Law and Justice. These amendments dealt with minor issues, you can say details, but also important ones. One amendment referred to a change in the model of keeping the VAT account. The bank will only open one account for all settlement accounts - this amendment was accepted by the committee - so that these accounts will not be multiplied by opening them for each account that the trader runs at the bank. The exception will be the National Bank of Poland. The second amendment, also tabled by Mr Sylvester Tulayev, is an amendment introducing the possibility to serve provisions using the electronic communication system or the electronic communication system. It concerns provisions on the authorisation of the head of the tax office to transfer the funds collected in the separate VAT account to another account held by the trader in the same bank. This amendment was also accepted by the committee. It remains to be summed up, to thank the government on behalf of the committee for a really good job, for a bill that will, as a result, give us a good increase in revenue to the budget, will, I think, end effectively the issue of these VAT carousels. I move to the High House at the same time to adopt the attached bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u231	Thank you. Mr Włodzimierz Nykiel, Civic Platform, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u232	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present an opinion on the draft law amending the Act on VAT on goods and services and certain other laws. The split payment mechanism introduced in the project may be an effective instrument to combat tax evasion in relation to the tax on goods and services, as the tax paid at the price determined by the supplier or service provider will be credited to the separate VAT account and from it to the tax authority. However, it should be stressed that the rules contained in the project also have shortcomings. They may have a negative impact on the financial liquidity of traders, since the taxable person who is a supplier or service provider will be free to own only the net amount of the receivable received, as the amount of the tax which is part of the price will be frozen in a separate VAT account. Moreover, it should be borne in mind that the introduction of a split payment mechanism will cost both businesses and banks. The Ministry of Finance in the explanatory memorandum of the project indicates that the application of the split payment mechanism will be voluntary. This is true, but only with regard to the buyer, because from the supplier's point of view it is difficult to say on a voluntary basis. It must be stressed that the buyer has the sole initiative to apply the split payment. As a result of a unilateral decision by the buyer on the supplier's side, there is a need whether the obligation to pay in such a form, which is often detrimental to it, for its liquidity, becomes necessary. The Ministry of Finance itself acknowledges in its justification that the financial consequences may be serious for suppliers. The granting of an exclusive initiative to the purchaser using a split payment mechanism may lead to an increase in the number of cash transactions, of course within the limits applicable in this respect, and the bureaucracy of business-to-business relations. Uncertainty as to whether or not the purchaser will benefit from the split payment mechanism means that the supplier is deprived of the possibility of forecasting its financial situation and pursuing a rational financial policy. Currently, the turnover, as we know, often takes place without a written contract. Following the introduction of a split payment mechanism, the supplier may seek to formalise the contractual relationship with the buyer by concluding a written agreement in order to increase the predictability of its financial situation, its financial flows. Therefore, it should be considered whether it is appropriate to authorise the supplier directly at the stage of the contract to be able to expect a binding declaration from the purchaser whether or not he intends to use this option. This would eliminate one of the most important shortcomings of this regulation. The desire to exclude the right of the purchaser to use the split payment option may lead to stigmatisation of the trader in the market as a potential fraud, even if the trader has never committed fraud before, there is no fraud in the plans, but is simply forced to ensure that his financial situation is predictable in order to maintain liquidity. Paradoxically, however, such an effort may undermine the credibility of the supplier in the market, dissuading the buyer from entering into a transaction. Contrary to the claims of the Ministry of Finance, honest suppliers may not want to receive VAT payments, because the funds collected in this account are theoretically the funds owned by the supplier, but at the same time the resources which are heavily restricted. It should also be noted that the impact of the introduction of this institution will be better managed by larger actors with resources to benefit from expert advice and analysis and with greater potential to mitigate the negative impact on financial flows by obtaining external funding. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u233	Thank you very much. Mr Sitarski, Kukiz, 15, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u234	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of the Kukiz club15 regarding the government draft law amending the Act on VAT on goods and services and certain other laws, print No 1864. I would like to present what will be the mechanism of split payments according to the proposed law. The buyer will transfer only the net amount to the seller's basic account and the calculated tax on goods and services, which is part of the gross amount, will be transferred to the special VAT account. And here I'm going to try to list the raised doubts about the bill. The use of split payment will limit access to funds frozen in the VAT account, thereby reducing the company's liquidity. The situation becomes particularly complicated for the buyer, for example. an excess amount will be transferred to the VAT account. There will be situations where bilateral commitments will be regulated in split payment mode in full, in part or in general. Which institutions will keep VAT accounts? Do banks and payment institutions have to carry them out? Here the question arises as to the extent to which they will be responsible for the collection of funds. Is the rate of the funds collected in the VAT account assumed? I will present some questions and comments that I have received as part of the drafting of my position on the bill. In a letter addressed to me by Jastrzębska Spółka Węglowa on the impact of changes in VAT settlements on the activities of public administration and companies, I was informed that when the split payment mechanism is applied, the moment of collection of the tax is shifted for a moment of payment. Consequently, the implementation of the split payment model should in principle be linked to a change in the timing of the tax obligation and the deadline for payment of the supply tax and the provision of services covered by it at the moment of receipt of payment. With this mechanism, account must be taken of the cost of implementing the different options, it will be necessary to adapt the payment and accounting systems to the new needs. The completion of the tax system will benefit from the creation of an electronic invoice database. Tax offices should have a database of entities involved in activities that have the characteristics of tax fraud. Such a base would also protect honest entities from the danger of unwittingly entering into a transaction with such an entity. In the position sent to me by the Polish Petroleum Concerto Orlen, the company president informs me that the main advantage of the application of split payment over other mechanisms, among others. The reverse payment or central invoice register is the fact that it is a systemic, preventive and long-term solution. The split payment model will prevent abuse already at the transaction stage. The split payment mechanism should be considered as an institution influencing the sealing of the tax system and as an effective tool against VAT fraudsters. At this point, I will also use the opinion of the Chief Director of the Main Mining Institute, who presented me with problems in writing and pointed out difficult issues related to the settlement of tax on goods and services, which need to be improved. Here is the problem of time-consuming tax formalities. For example, the Institute prepares and sends two VAT and VAT-EU declarations on a monthly basis and a single control file. All this concerns the settlement of a single tax on goods and services, which, with a large number of documents, makes it necessary to devote a considerable amount of time to the preparation and reconciliation of data in order to draw up and submit a declaration to the tax office. It will also be a problem for large units to move away from the quarterly clearing system, which may also result in difficulties. Clear and transparent rules are also lacking. The existing VAT rules are often unclear, incomprehensible and require frequent use of tax rulings or court case law issued by tax chambers. Unfavourable way of deduction of VAT using the proportion of sales and the pre-ratio of sales. An entity such as an institute, which in the structure of revenue one third of its revenue is obtained from non-VAT subsidies, has lost the right to deduct VAT by 30% compared to the situation of 1 January 2016, resulting in an increase in operating costs, including the transfer of higher VAT in activities carried out to an unchanged extent and the absence of an increase in sales, etc., etc. Regarding the fact that the head of the IRS will have 25 days to pay back the excess VAT... An application for reimbursement to a VAT account will be able to be submitted by any clearing taxable person in a split payment system. Here we are talking about the money deficit on the Polish market, a multi-billion-dollar deficit, and this deficit hole will be enlarged even further. You know, there's a lot of comments. As much as you need to look at it, [[♪ The bell ♪]] support any amendments that will be tabled and consider whether this solution will really benefit the Polish State. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u235	Thank you very much. Mr Mirosław Pampuch, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u236	Dear Marshal, Minister! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! On behalf of the Poselski Club I present the position of the club regarding the government draft law amending the act on tax on goods and services and some other laws. The purpose of the bill submitted is to stiffen the system of value added tax and to ensure greater stability of VAT receipts as well as to prevent tax avoidance. According to the proposed solutions, a new tool is to be introduced, so-called. split payment mechanism. This mechanism boils down to dividing the payments for the goods or services delivered into two amounts: net, paid by the purchaser to the account of the supplier or service provider, and the amount of tax to be entered in a separate VAT account. From this separate VAT account the taxable person will be able to pay the tax on goods and services as well as pay his VAT obligations to the tax office. The proposed solution assumes the buyer's voluntary principle in the application of the split payment mechanism. This also means that it can be used in a selective manner, i.e. not every invoice must be regulated using a split payment mechanism. It is the purchaser who decides which invoice he will pay using this mechanism and on which supplier he will apply it. It must be borne in mind that the purchaser's decision unfortunately affects the supplier of goods or the provider of services. The seller will be forced, and therefore obliged, to participate in this mechanism. The Ministry claims that the draft law provides for facilitations to encourage taxpayers to use the split payment mechanism. One of these facilitations is to be faster - i.e. within 25 days - VAT refund. However, let us remember that VAT is refunded within 25 days at the request of the taxable person. In such a situation, unfortunately, the Ministry misses the truth by referring to the additional benefit to the taxpayer resulting from the application of the split payment mechanism. Of course, the Ministry has provided for other mechanisms or facilitations for taxpayers using this mechanism. In my opinion, however, they do not compensate for the negative effects that may affect entrepreneurs. First of all, in this mechanism, the funds received for VAT due will not be used by companies for ongoing non-VAT settlement operations - including public-law debt operations; this was a call for many entrepreneurs to pay public-law claims from this account as well. Consequently, this law may lead to a reduction in the liquidity of companies. Thus, companies may have to bear additional costs associated with the need to borrow to cover liquidity losses or the loss of the management benefits of the available funds. Moreover, an obvious complication of the payment process and among others. the need to monitor the receipts for VAT own account and to make possible adjustments resulting from erroneous transactions will require an increase in the cash and time spent on accounting services. Let us also remember that companies will be forced to bear additional costs to adapt their IT and accounting systems. The Ministry assumes that, under certain circumstances, the taxable person will be able to benefit from the funds collected in the VAT account with the permission of the head of the tax office. However, the problem is that provisions on this issue can be issued within 60 days. Such a long period is clearly unsatisfactory for entrepreneurs. Despite these reservations, due to the fact that the proposed project aims to eliminate the VAT gap, the actual problem facing our financial system, the Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u237	Thank you. Ms Genowefa Tokarska, Polish People's Party, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u238	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I present my position on the report of the committee on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Tax on Goods and Services and some other laws, namely 1864 and 1962. The Committee on Public Finance analysed the project, accepted several editorial amendments and adopted two substantive amendments which were not of great importance and decisive importance for the project. The amendments adopted concern the use of the electronic system for the correspondence of interested parties and the possibility for the holder of the bill of account to request the opening of more than one VAT account for the accounts held. Recalling the project's assumptions, it should be stressed that an additional mechanism is introduced in the VAT system, i.e. the split payment mechanism, which is, as my predecessors have already said, that the payment for the goods is divided into the net value of the sale paid by the buyer to the supplier's clearing account and the amount of VAT that is paid on a special account in a bank or cooperative credit and savings bank in Polish currency. The accounts according to this project do not require separate contracts: they will be settled on general terms, so without any additional fees and fees. The split payment mechanism is intended to ensure better transparency of VAT settlements and this is the main objective of this project. The proposal assumes - what is important and what should be underlined - the voluntary principle in the application of this mechanism by taxpayers and the tax authorities would not be able to access the account without the taxpayer's consent. On the other hand, there are numerous incentives for taxpayers to use the split payment mechanism. These incentives include: non-application to these taxable persons of penalties resulting from the VAT Act; the fact that the provisions on joint and several liability will not apply; the faster settlement of refunds of excess VAT or the non-payment of a higher amount of interest for late payment in certain cases of settlement. By keeping a separate VAT account, the taxable person will be able to demonstrate and prove that he is carrying out tax settlements with due care. I would say that this is quite an important argument to keep accounts through this special account. Unfortunately, liquidity problems can be a billing instrument for split payments, especially for small businesses. The discussed project contains very numerous technical solutions for the operation and operation of VAT accounts, with many limitations. In conclusion, I would like to stress that the introduction of a split payment mechanism with this separate VAT account will lead to restrictions on the ownership by businesses of their funds, and in these small ones may cause problems and liquidity disruptions, but there are also arguments regarding the transparency of the accounts and every time the tax authorities have documented to the tax authorities by the trader that the settlement is conducted carefully and reliably. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote in favour of this project. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u252	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you for all the comments in this first part and some questions to this law. This project seems to be the best possible compromise of business, economy and fiscal interest. It is clear that, by the very nature of the split payment mechanism, there must be some restriction on the right to dispose of the funds collected in that account. Otherwise, the split payment mechanism would not be itself, it would not carry out those basic functions which were provided for in the European Commission's 2010 documents, where this mechanism was outlined. With regard to the issue of derogations in the European Union, we are in dialogue with the European Union, with the Commission. It is necessary to distinguish between the draft currently under consideration, where we have confirmation by the European Commission that it does not require a derogation... [[Voice from the room: Officially.]] On the other hand, if we extend its operation to mandatory settlements in a split payment mechanism for specific sectors, we need a derogation for these specific sectors. At least today there is such a state of knowledge, and in such a dialogue we are with the European Commission. As regards the issue of effects and whether our OSRs are overestimated or overestimated and how we are supposed to deal with the effects of this law, I would like to say that this law is part of a series of major structural and legislative changes related to VAT sealing. The first thing, of course, was the electronicization of the JPK, which is that entrepreneurs - the biggest ones - submit the JPK monthly to the IRS. We extend this to all entrepreneurs, we add some functionality in a short time and we will have so-called. the central register of invoices. These are things known in Europe. This is the first element. The second element is the STIR-owska Act, i.e. the IT system of the clearing house, which is to supply the treasury with data about financial flows, so that behaviorally it can be analyzed and invented even predictively carousels before they even bring money abroad. Another element will be the split payment, which has a different effect. It has e.g. also the effect of protecting the interests of the trader and safeguarding the market against unfair competition and unfair VAT taxpayers. These are very difficult to estimate the results of individual components of this structure. There are also indirect effects, e.g. such as economic development or the fact that, as we can see, after sealing Orlen fuels has income and can invest. Where are these revenues and where are these investments for the last 8, 10 years? These are the costs of VAT fraud today unquoted and it is difficult to quantify for the future the profit for the economy, not for the fiscal, contrary to what is said here from this parliamentary tribune, the profit for the economy due to the sealing of the system and the level playing field for entrepreneurs. These things are very difficult to quantify and the whole system must unfortunately be assessed as a whole as a source of state revenue and VAT sealing. As far as credit is concerned, these measures, if it is not necessary to use them for VAT obligations, will constitute assets of the trader, will be released in accordance with the procedures laid down in the Act and will be available to the bank bailiff or non-bank bailiff. As regards the deadline for VAT reimbursement, I would like to remind you that Poland is in a good European average, and I will even say that we are returning more quickly than many European countries, those with more mature: administrations, jurisdictions and fiscals. As regards interest rates, the rate of the VAT account is directly linked, in accordance with the provisions of this project, with the interest rate of this leading account. So there's no arbitration on interest. As regards the general remark concerning the freezing of money on the VAT account, I will repeat once again that this is an immanent feature of the split payment mechanism. We've got mechanisms to mitigate this effect. First of all, VAT can be prepaid and you can get a discount account. This is the first thing that this money isn't lying around and wasting away. Secondly, you can pay your counterparties for this VAT. And these are the elements that mythize in legislative form. In an operational form, there will be a programme of loans, restructuring loans to improve its liquidity, to improve the business' economic capacity from one of the state-owned banks 100 percent, and this programme already dedicated synonymously to entrepreneurs who present their problem will be addressed. The details will be presented soon. In our view, however, the sum of these solutions entitles us to introduce this mechanism, especially in the voluntary version. And here I would like to stress once again that if an entrepreneur does not want to use this mechanism, he declares it to his customers and these customers simply decide whether they are trading with this counterparty or not. This is a left-over market game, business decisions. On the other hand, if the taxpayer wants to strictly protect his interest in the risk of being involved in the merry-go-round, he will be able to benefit from this mechanism and protect himself from the negative consequences of these events. So I think the project deserves support. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u260	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I submit to the High House a report on the work of the committee on 7 November on the draft law amending the Law on Specific Solutions for the Implementation of the Budget Law for 2017. The legal solutions proposed by the government in this project make it possible to finance the implementation adopted by the government, in agreement with Poles, of a programme to improve the social situation and economic development in our country. During the committee's work, three amendments were tabled to the government proposal. Following their discussion, the amendments were adopted by a large majority. These amendments increase the transparency of the rules and improve the spending of funds. Amendment 1 provides for an amendment to paragraph 1. Article 1 16a. This article provides the possibility to provide the National Radio and Television Council with compensation of PLN 980 million for the proceeds of subscription fees lost in 2010 and 2017 for the dismissal of persons, inter alia. those who are over 75 years of age, disabled, receiving low pensions. The full catalogue of exempt persons shall be governed by the Act on subscription fees. The government proposes that these funds be used to carry out a public service mission by public broadcasting units. The Commission's positive opinion leads to the replacement of the expression "expenditure' by the expression "costs'. Where public service broadcasting undertakings carry out public-funded tasks and settle according to an accrual-based, cost-based method, the proposed provision needs to be amended. Amendment 2 concerns Article. 16c project to create a special-purpose reserve for the provision of health prevention offices in schools. An amount of 88 217 000 was provided to ensure improvements in the quality and availability of healthcare services. zł. Local government units will be able to apply for a grant to create offices in schools for which they are an authority to the competent voivodeship. A grant to equip the health prevention cabinet with equipment in schools subject to ministers will be granted by the competent minister and other public schools will be awarded by the voivode at their request. The amendment to this amendment excludes the application of Article. 170 Public Finance Act, which sets out the deadlines for changing the amounts of special grants to tasks commissioned by local government units. Amendment 3 also excludes the application of Article in 2017. 170 to the amount planned for educational services in part 85 of the voivod's budgets. High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I request that these amendments be adopted in accordance with the opinion of the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u312	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! A number of questions concerning the amendment of the Budget Act for 2017 concerned matters submitted by the Minister of Culture. On the other hand, I would like to denounce these rumors, because, first of all, public television is public because it is financed by public money and pursues public objectives, which means that we have agreed that television has a whole catalogue of tasks that it must perform under the obligation of the State. The State must therefore ensure that these tasks are financed. These tasks are called a public mission. If in 2010 we introduced a whole range of reductions that led to the fact that the level of funding has significantly decreased - and today it is about 977 million PLN, that is almost as much as we put in the budget: 980 million PLN - if there is a situation that the Prime Minister of the Civic Platform government urges Poles not to pay their subscription, which started a very strong trend of leaving Poles from paying their subscription... [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]] ...then we have to ask ourselves how we want to keep the public media if we want to keep the public media, because I feel that, many times, I have been so impressed by the debates conducted in the Parliamentary Committees that we need public television, including. so that she can carry out public tasks. Public tasks are those tasks which are not carried out by commercial media and which do not pay off to commercial broadcasters, commercial broadcasters, e.g. These are tasks related to the dissemination of high culture. [[Mr Iwona Śledzińska-Katarasińska: Martyniuk.]] These are also tasks that very rarely appear in commercial media, because advertisers do not pay for advertising during such programs or pay very little because such programs are less attractive. On the other hand, we have a duty to direct the programme to all citizens, not just so-called. a commercial group, that is, the one to which advertising is addressed. We cannot live under the dictatorship of advertisers who will tell us exactly what program should be on TV. As a state, we should be able to compensate for this group, which is not interested in the commercial programme, through the possibility of watching the non-commercial programme. As far as the legality of these measures is concerned, I remind you that when we entered the European Union and committed that we would not grant public aid to various companies, companies or institutions that operate on the same market as other institutions, we made an exception - we and almost all the countries of the European Union, and, as far as I know, all the countries of the European Union - just for the public media. Why? That is why - as I said earlier - someone must carry out this mission not under commercial dictation. Therefore, we have the right to reach a ceiling of about PLN 3 billion in terms of public aid. The European Union or state aid legislation is in fact not interested in where this money comes from, whether it comes from a subscription or from a state grant - in different countries it is different - only how we spend it and whether it does not affect competitiveness in the same market where other players operate, for example. other commercial televisions. It must be remembered that public television is the leading broadcaster in Poland, it occupies almost 1/3 of the market, if not more. The remaining part of the cake is divided between two other large broadcasters, and I would like to point out that the television budget is two or three times smaller than those commercial broadcasters, and yet public television maintains almost 10 thematic channels. You have accusations primarily for publicists or information, for example. On TVP channel one, the whole program lasts about an hour, and there is a continuous broadcast of about 20 hours. In addition, there are regional centres which inform about events and activities that take place in the regions and which are not reported by any other television, unless these are sensational activities. And we have a duty to spend money on all of this. It is not that we have spent 1 billion PLN on television, because we pulled them out of the hat, and before that we gave almost 1 billion, as one of the Members said, because we did not give them... There's been a loan, a loan on commercial terms, it's 3.5%. [[Paulin Hennig-Kloska: With a 2-year penalty.]] Therefore, television will pay off this loan because it has such a duty... [[Paulin Hennig-Kloska: In the next term of office, you will lose your power.]] ...and 1 billion is public aid, which is not provided directly to television, but is provided to all public media, i.e. Polish Radio, all regional stations that are closest to people. And that's the money for them too. I wanted to point out one thing here: It is not that we are pulling some money from the government, giving it to television, and since then we have power over television. No. We, ladies and gentlemen, are handing over this money to the National Radio and Television Council, [[Joy in the Chamber]] a constitutional body which treats that money exactly the same as the proceeds from the subscription. And it is the National Council of Radio and Television that separates them, by resolution it decides who and how much money it will get. [[Whose national council is it?]] Therefore, such allegations are absurd. I also wanted to address the so-called issue. an algorithm that has been raised. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u328	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Izbo! The inspector will carry out his tasks with the help of the Office of Internal Surveillance. One of the proposed changes to the project allows the Minister to supervise the process of transforming the structures of today's Office of the Home Affairs of the Police into an isolated unit, the Office of the Internal Affairs of the Police, and on the other hand provides support to the Police Headquarters in the process of transforming this office. Given that the Minister would be given the authority to cast the Chief of Staff and the Deputy Chief of Police and the Border Guard's Internal Affairs Office respectively, it is necessary to appoint a Minister as disciplinary officer to the Head of Internal Affairs of the Police and the Border Guard Office respectively. An important amendment is the possibility for the Police's Office of Home Affairs to carry out the task of identifying and combating crimes against the economic turnover committed to the detriment of the Police and Border Guard. In our view, the minister's political responsibility for the activities of his services should be based on real and enforceable competences. The bill submitted is intended to introduce a new quality in the supervision of services. In a professionally run large-scale company, the director responsible for his department is fully responsible for its operation, and this responsibility arises from having tools that enable him to supervise the work of the department he manages. We cannot allow the State not to react to specific issues that undermine public confidence in state services, services that are one of the pillars of statehood. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u332	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 concerning the Government's draft law amending the Act on certain powers of employees of the office serving the Minister responsible for Home Affairs and officials and servants of the offices supervised by that Minister and certain other laws. As stated in the explanatory memorandum, the proposed solution aims to implement the standard of civil oversight of uniformed public policy services. In addition, the need to implement it should be justified by the creation of mechanisms to strengthen the existing instruments of supervision of the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration over controlled services, which are intended to benefit from the fact that these entities benefit from a very wide range of statutory powers which provide for the possibility of interference in the rights and freedoms of citizens, as well as the performance of tasks liable to cause certain corruption risks. These are identified here by the applicants as areas of potential abuse. As far as change is concerned, we are dealing with, among other things. with the establishment of a new civilian supervisory authority, i.e. the so-called. an inspector of internal surveillance under the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The activities of the inspector are to strengthen the supervision of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of uniform formations, the inspector will provide the Minister with information and material obtained as a result of the tasks carried out. The main task of the inspector is to support actions aimed at enforcing high standards of service operation, verifying candidates for senior posts in the services, monitoring and revealing behaviour that violate the principles of professional ethics in the services. There will also be an Internal Surveillance Bureau with the inspector. The inspector is to be appointed and cancelled by the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration. There are also formal requirements. The office is to be composed of seconded police officers, Border Guards as office inspectors, assisted by seconded BOR officers and firefighters of the State Fire Department. These are office experts. The Minister of Home Affairs and Administration will report annually to the Sejm and the Senate on the application of operational control by the Office of Internal Surveillance, and the existing cells for the internal affairs of the Police and Border Guard will be organisationally separated from the headquarters of these services and will become organisational units respectively: the Office of Internal Affairs of the Police and the Office of Home Affairs of the Border Guard, and the commanders of these units will become bodies. Ladies and gentlemen! There are very important powers that we are now granting, whether this law grants, to the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration. We understand the need to sort things out in uniform services, especially the police, because there are many different types of cases that need to be sorted out and clarified. I also know from the autopsy that there are a lot of cases like this. The hearing at the subcommittee meeting has convinced us that there is a need for certain external control mechanisms, and yesterday's hearing before the Amber Gold Commission has clearly shown what the situation may look like when it is left on its own, only internal control. So that maybe this is the way to eliminate all kinds of pathology, but at the same time it is the way to give the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration very much competence. This problem will always crash into the person who holds this position. If it is a person who will act pro-stateally, he will be a unit focused on actually acting and cleansing this environment or explaining some annoying issues, then we will be calm about it. However, if there is a person in such a position who wishes to use it politically, unfortunately these powers will be able to be used so. This is the danger that this bill brings with it. Nevertheless, we would be able to support this law, but, Minister - and here without offense - we have some doubts as to whether you are the person who gives us the guarantee of using this law correctly. Unfortunately, these actions, which are being undertaken in the part of the ministry that is subject to you, have for us the mark of switching the supervisory authority from the body to which he is to supervise. A little bit in this case, as the proverb says, the tail spins the dog. These doubts remain with us, but they are clear, and for this reason we will decide how to vote tomorrow. Thank you very much.
