So far this surveillance has been illusory and largely theoretical. The draft law prepared by the government and clarified by the work of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs - it has indeed been clarified in a number of provisions - seeks to implement the standards of civil oversight of uniformed services, public policy services. I would say, in fact, that it finally establishes civilian supervision of uniformed services, because so far the supervision in the scope referred to by the law was that these cells, which you call departments - the internal affairs offices - were subject to who was to be supervised. Self-supervising is rather bad, it is subjective supervision, and it must be external to be objective. In the current state of law, the Minister of Home Affairs, despite its formal sovereignty over these formations, has in fact significantly limited possibilities for autonomous and direct examination of all information and signals about negative phenomena in these services. The need for mechanisms to strengthen and implement existing supervisory instruments also arises - I would also like to stress very strongly - you interpret it in the contrary, as you do more conveniently, from a purely political position, in order to attack the government - the use by supervised services of statutory powers, which provide for the possibility of significant interference in civil rights and freedoms, as well as the performance of tasks liable to cause corruption risks. It's written in the bill's explanatory memorandum. And that is what I would like to stress very strongly: if we are dealing with such an advantage of instruments, competences, opportunities for services, and these classified instruments, these services must be particularly supervised so that there is no abuse here. It is about checking the regularity of all those activities to which the services are entitled. After all, yesterday we adopted in one of the amendments a provision which says precisely that the Minister responsible for Home Affairs is to control e.g. the field of operational and exploratory activities as regards the regularity of their conduct. The Minister will not perform surgical and exploratory activities, we are not creating a new service. The Internal Surveillance Office is not a new service, it is just an instrument, it is a tool that will allow the Minister to carry out the responsible supervisory tasks entrusted to him. This separation of the two Internal Affairs Offices - in the Police and Border Guard - is about to lead to the disclosure of supervision. These offices will be subject to the Home Affairs Minister, not the Chiefs. I would like to draw your attention in this discussion to another very important aspect, and I also spoke about it yesterday at the committee meeting. The Minister of Home Affairs not only supervises the commandants, but also oversees the services - the services that are under his supervision under the rules. The Minister responsible for Home Affairs will therefore be supervised, inter alia. compliance with the standards of operation of the Police, Border Guard, the Office for Government Protection, the State Protection Service in the future, and the State Fire Department in connection with the need to ensure respect for human and citizen rights and freedoms and compliance with the principles of professional ethics by the officers of these formations, as well as the correctness of implementation, as I have just said, of operational and cognitive activities and investigations and disciplinary proceedings - regularity. The proper conduct of these cases, the regularity of the proceedings. I answer one of the questions right away. No one will replace, for example. disciplinary activities, disciplinary proceedings, only the supervision of their regularity. Recently, there has been a little, not even a little, public discussion on this subject, and to settle easily, but when there are no mechanisms to respond, it is necessary to create these mechanisms in order to be responsible for this. This is, ladies and gentlemen, of course, the great responsibility of every minister responsible for home affairs, regardless of what names are behind this function today, will hide tomorrow or the day after tomorrow. But it is necessary to establish this courageous civilian supervision mechanism, the real supervision of uniform services, but finally in Poland, after 27 years of democracy, not to pretend that there is no problem. So far, everyone thought there was no problem. The problem is and it needs to be solved, and we just have the courage to face it. Key solutions for this project, in order to implement the Minister's supervision, were foreseen on several levels, as was mentioned in the club speeches. As a result, a coherent system of surveillance of uniformed services is being created. First of all, the Act introduces appropriate arrangements for the competence of the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration - when I use this name, I am of course thinking of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, as regards naming and the Law on Departments - defining for the first time in a transparent manner the scope of his supervision and the powers he has in this regard. These changes are complemented by the creation of a new supervisory authority, directly subordinate to the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration, i.e. it is the inspector of internal surveillance, and it is equipped with powers enabling it, as I stress once again, to assist the Minister in carrying out his duty to supervise his subordinate services. One of the most important tasks of the inspector of internal surveillance will be to identify and detect intentional, prosecuted by accusations of public crimes - and to prevent them - and tax offences committed by officials of services supervised by the Minister and their employees, i.e. phenomena of a clearly negative nature which undermine social trust not only in these services but also in the institutions of the State in general. To the institution of the state. The internal surveillance inspector will be required to present immediately the information and material obtained as a result of the implementation of the tasks which may be relevant to that oversight by the Minister. The Act also introduces appropriate amendments because they are necessary as an instrument, as a way of achieving the objective at organisational level. In the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the Office of Internal Supervision will be set up - which arouses such emotions, and that will not, once again, any new service - through which the inspector of internal supervision will carry out his tasks. This office will be an organisational cell separated for this purpose by the Ministry, so it will not constitute anything else, any new, separate service, but merely an internal organisational structure in the Ministry of Home Affairs. In addition to the internal surveillance officer himself and his alternates, the Internal Surveillance Office will be seconded to carry out the tasks of police officers and Border Guard officers, as well as seconded officials of the Government Protection Office and firefighters of the State Fire Department as experts. The essence of this solution is based on the knowledge of the seconded officers - we will invite the best officers to do so - in the internal functioning of the home service, which is intended to facilitate the achievement of the objectives set before the inspector of internal surveillance. The draft law thus limits to the necessary minimum the financial consequences of the functioning of the Office of Internal Supervision for the State budget, and they are very clearly defined in specific precise figures in the draft law. The provisions of this law also assume that internal affairs cells in the Police and Border Guard, which today operate within these services, will be isolated and will become independent organisational units within these services. At the same time, the nature of the tasks of the new units will remain unchanged. The Chiefs of the Office of the Interior of the Police, the Office of the Home Affairs of the Border Guard, and their alternates will be appointed by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. The point is that they should not be subjected to commandants who oversee their services, because they must be - for the sake of objectivity of their actions - just a service situated, in a competent sense, outside these formations. In the new formula, these offices will of course cooperate in this respect with the inspector of internal surveillance. At the same time, the scope of supervision exercised by the Minister will be extended to supervise the performance of tasks by the Police Office and the Border Guard Office, whose commandants will be required to provide the Minister with information and material which may be of great importance to the Minister of Supervision. I would like to stress, once again, that the solutions proposed in the draft will, on the one hand, implement the supervision exercised by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration of his subordinate uniform services and, on the other hand, allow effective and objective verification of the information on irregularities in their operation and the immediate taking of appropriate supervisory and corrective action. This is the first systemic approach for many years to the question of the Minister responsible for home affairs to supervise public policy services, which will also guarantee practical tools for its implementation. It is worth remembering that public policy officials must meet the highest moral and ethical standards which constitute and must be the foundation of public confidence in the institutions of the state. The proposed solutions aim primarily to eliminate from the ranks of services supervised by the Minister responsible for the internal affairs of persons who undermine this social confidence through their improper conduct, behaviour, through activities that are not in line with standards, and it happens that also with legal provisions. I am very surprised in this context - I had to remind you once again, in a nutshell, of what this law is all about - these attacks, this opposition. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u370	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position towards the Government's draft law amending the Act on certain powers of servants of the office serving the Minister responsible for Home Affairs and officials and servants of the offices supervised by that Minister and certain other laws. The Minister for Home Affairs and Administration shall be responsible for all activities of the uniformed services, both those in their competence and for abuse and pathology. He answers to the whole nation, citizens, voters. Like everyone else, he is accounted for by his effectiveness. He is blamed for the scandals and irregularities in the police or other formations, as well as for the delay in explaining these scandals. However, there is no doubt that he has not so far had the appropriate instruments to supervise his subordinate services. This draft law provides for the provision of this instrument to the appropriate minister. By changing the way of supervision of operational and cognitive activities, it is given the opportunity to access information so that it can perform its function better and more efficiently. It will increase its impact on the proper functioning of the services and will be able to react more quickly to the events. Situations such as the tragedy that took place at the Wrocław police station confirm that the adoption of new regulations is needed. That it is necessary to increase control in order to eliminate such abuses and, above all, prevent them. It is worth noting that the novel does not change the competence of individual bodies. The bill is not intended to involve uniformed services in a political dispute or take command. In conclusion, I share the view that civilian oversight of services must be realistic and that the regulations contained in this project serve this purpose. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u373	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Wojciech Murdzek, the Law and Justice Club, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u374	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The draft law amending the Corporate Income Tax Act, print no. 1930, provides that the Minister of Development and Finance will be able to make public CIT taxpayers' data resulting from the tax returns submitted. The scope of the data that will be available to the public is as follows: the name and VAT of the taxable person, the tax year, the amount of revenue and the cost of obtaining revenue, the income of the taxable person, the tax base and the CIT tax. According to the project, the public disclosure of tax data relates to taxable persons for whom, for the tax year for which the information is to be published, the value of revenue exceeded the amount in PLN corresponding to the equivalent of EUR 50 million, as well as taxable persons having the tax status of the group, regardless of the level of income achieved by that group. As is apparent from the OSR project, the condition concerning the above-mentioned condition is fulfilled. the level of revenue meets around 2 000. taxpayers. In addition, there are about 60 tax groups of capital. The proposed provisions provide for the possibility for the taxable person to submit a request for the deletion or rectification of the data, which will include inter alia. justification. If such a request is not taken into account, the Minister for Development and Finance will issue a negative decision if this would result in non-compliance with the facts. The data to be made public will be updated periodically. The project provides for quarterly updates as at the first day of the month in which the quarter of the calendar year ends. It should be stressed that the draft shows that the tax data disclosed to the public will not infringe the tax confidentiality rules. The new regulations will apply from the tax year which started before 1 January 2018 and will end after 31 December 2017. The draft also proposes that the data of taxable persons arising from the statements for earlier tax years, starting from the tax year beginning after December 2011. This deadline is linked to the period of limitation of tax obligations provided for in the Tax Order. The solutions proposed in the draft law are to enter into force on 1 January 2018. The regulations provided for in the draft law are part of the actions undertaken both in Poland and in the international arena. They are an example of a solution that responds to the global problem of tax avoidance and erosion of the tax base of the countries where the income is generated. This problem has been noticed, among others. by the OECD, the G20 or the European Union. The project is in line with the trend of increasing citizens' awareness of the financial basis of the State's activities, as well as the actions undertaken by international bodies. The work on the annual accounts, consolidated accounts and related reports of certain types of entities concerning the disclosure of income tax information by certain entities and branches is being carried out within the European Union, as already mentioned by the Minister. These solutions are therefore intended to support action to combat aggressive tax planning, which the European Commission has recognised as one of the key political priorities of the European Union in implementing the strategy for a fair and effective corporate tax system in the European Union. According to the justification of the draft law amending the Corporate Income Tax Act, the solution introduced by this project, which provides for the publication of tax data on individual taxpayers, is already in place in these countries. I think that being in a group of countries that are the first to introduce new solutions makes our country stand out. Therefore, I think that further proceding will result in this project of supporters and will be carried out in an efficient and efficient manner. Thank you very much.

As the Minister said, it is 60% CIT. However, the introduction of high standards in tax proceedings and the expectation of increasing taxpayers' awareness must be a mutual action, and therefore also a government. Much greater results can be achieved when business conditions are constantly improved, not just by imposing additional obligations. However, from the last published report of the World Bank Doing Business, the conditions for doing business in Poland have deteriorated. Poland fell in this ranking from 24th to 27th place in 2017. Also in the ranking of the competitiveness of the economy, published by the World Economic Forum, our country fell to 39th place from 36th place. In the next ranking, the transparency of the law, we take 118th place in 137 countries. These are worrying phenomena which require deep reflection, detailed analysis and concrete government action to improve this situation. By appealing for such action, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club is in favour of directing the bill to further legislative work. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u397	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The draft law on the amendment of the excise duty Act submitted to the Sejm introduces taxation of new categories of products with excise duty - electronic cigarette liquids and novelty products as from 1 January 2018. Both electronic cigarettes using liquid for the consumption of steam and novelty products containing tobacco in their composition, which, when heated, provides nicotine in the form of steam, are a substitute for traditional tobacco products, and therefore the main objective of the project is to guarantee equal tax treatment for traders active in the traditional tobacco industry and entities in the electronic cigarettes and novelty industries. We also expect that the measures taken on the basis of the proposed rules will affect the regulation of the market for electronic cigarettes and novelty products and increase consumer safety. Thanks to the excise mark, the banner applied to the packaging of e-cigarettes and novelty products, consumers will be able to verify the legality of the product themselves at the time of purchase. The proposed project assumes an electronic cigarette liquid rate of 50 gr per ml and 141,29 PLN per kilogram and 31,41% weighted average retail selling price of smoking tobacco, approx. PLN 300 per kilogram. These are moderate rates against the background of European countries. Today, as many as 13 EU countries have taxed innovative products and nine countries have taxed electronic cigarette liquid. Against the expectations of the e-cigarette and novelty industry, the bill provides for exemption from the obligation to mark excise marks for a period of one year of electronic cigarette and novelty products on the market. In addition, the project introduces a three-month transitional period for obtaining a tax warehouse permit. It is also worth mentioning that in the course of the work, already in the Council of Ministers, the rate of excise duty on e-cigarettes was reduced in relation to the original project from PLN 0.7 to PLN 0.5. Although the e-cigarette market is a young but stable market, with a well-established number of users, which is already in the process of adapting to the new requirements of the Health Protection Act against the consequences of tobacco and tobacco products, i.e. the obligation to register products and the prohibition to sell on the Internet and to persons under the age of 18, as well as to prohibit the use of e-cigarettes in public places. Please also note that the above law treats electronic cigarettes and novel products as products linked to traditional tobacco products. According to the draft law amending the excise duty Act, electronic cigarette and novelty products will be taxed as substitute for tobacco products. As regards the risk of an increase in the price of newly taxed products, note that a traditional tobacco smoker, assuming he smokes a pack of cigarettes a day, spends about 5 000 each year. zł. On the other hand, the average consumer of e-cigarette fluid consumes about 1 l of e-cigarette per year. The value of 1 l of electronic cigarette liquid, including excise duty, will be approx. $1,600. So it's still the electronic cigarettes that will be more attractive. economically for the consumer. To sum up the above, I would like to stress that the draft law amending the excise duty law is in line with the need to align the business conditions in the tobacco industry and related products such as electronic cigarette fluid and novelty products. The project also aims to ensure that consumers supplying these products from legal sources are protected by excise marks and subject to customs-treasure checks, so that they can be easily distinguished from products without a guarantee of quality. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above, I propose the adoption of a draft law amending the excise duty law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u399	Dear Marshal! High Seym! I would like to present the opinion of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the Government's draft law amending the excise duty law, print no. 1963. The opinion is positive, we are in favour of referring this proposal to the Parliamentary Committee. The government justifies the entry into excise duty of two new categories of products, as the Minister has just said, electronic cigarette liquids and so-called. innovative products replacing traditional cigarettes and other tobacco products, the fact that the use by consumers of products not subject to excise duty is at the expense of the consumption of goods subject to excise duty. These products belong to the category of drugs which the State wishes to control better the production, movement and sale process. The introduction of an obligation to flag products replacing traditional tobacco products will facilitate the legality of the origin of this increasingly popular use, which is not irrelevant in terms of the quality of these products, their impact on the health of citizens and the prevention of illicit trade in such products. It should be agreed with the Government's argument that the progressive social process of replacing traditional tobacco products with their electronic counterparts applied in a different way than incineration leads consequently to a decrease in the level of consumers' use of excise goods, namely traditional tobacco products, and consequently to a decrease in the revenue of the state budget for this purpose. Although you can't see it yet, in the future such a trend may occur. The amendment of the Act should therefore be regarded as a necessary stabilisation of excise revenue for this reason, not as an increase in excise duty, especially since the tax dimension, as the Minister has just said, is to be only 50 gr per ml of electronic cigarette liquid, which is at a much lower rate than in other European countries. According to the justification, the additional revenue to the state budget for excise duty on electronic cigarette liquid will amount to about PLN 75 million per year. We should ensure that the state budget is sustainable and that not only is it possible to implement the necessary expenditure, and that expectations are very high in many areas, let us mention the need for health protection, but also in order to reduce the budget deficit and, consequently, to pursue a sustainable budget. Therefore, the search for income in those places where we are dealing with drugs replacing traditional cigarettes seems to be one of the least socially harmful methods. An important argument in the discussion is also that the taxation of products replacing traditional nicotine and tobacco products is introduced or is being introduced in many other European countries. The Minister mentioned that 13 countries were taxing innovative products, and for the nine countries to which Italy, Portugal, Finland, Hungary, Slovenia, Romania, Greece, Serbia, Latvia and Croatia belong, a tax on electronic cigarette liquid was introduced. The rates proposed in Poland do not differ in proportion from those in other countries, and as the Minister pointed out, they were even reduced at the stage of government work. The project has undergone a broad public consultation at the stage of government work, so it is not introduced without preparation, and its submission to the Sejm was preceded by in-depth impact analyses, which is also visible in a very comprehensive justification for the printing submitted no. 1963. For these reasons, we believe that work should be started in the Committee on Public Finance, where, we hope, there will also be a wide range of problems regarding the excise tax base, the reasons for the changes in excise revenue to the state budget and all the elements relating to the introduction of this tax, which will have an effect on companies involved in the marketing of such products, as well as for consumers. Thank you for your attention.

Large tobacco companies have had capital and many years of experience with tax warehouses, excise securities, as they have been taxpayers of this tax for years. For small and medium-sized Polish companies the introduction of a new tax at such a rapid pace means revolution, and for many of them the end of business. About 80% The Italian market has moved to the grey zone, more than 2 000 have fallen. trade shops, and the job lost close to 5.5 thousand. persons. Poland also has negative experiences with the increase in excise duty. They are illustrated by an example from 2011 and 2014. High increases in excise duty on ordinary cigarettes have led to the collapse of the legal market, a significant increase in the gray tobacco sector and a fall in budget revenue. In conclusion, the project in its shape should not be procedated. It should be noted that it has never been decided to introduce such a high rate of excise duty in relation to the starting price of the product. The excise duty on cigarettes was introduced in stages, and the rates increased gradually and not, as here, by leap. The Act is not about the health of Poles either. In addition, the taxation of these products with excise duty will result in their obligation to flag. The draft provides for exemptions for new products as for other excise goods in the case of intra-Community acquisitions and imports by travellers. It is therefore exempt from excise duty to import liquid for electronic cigarettes or novelty products imported in travellers' personal luggage, which is 17 years old, 50 ml of liquid for electronic cigarettes in air or sea transport, 10 ml in another transport or consignment and 0.04 kg of novel products in air or sea transport and 0.008 kg in another transport or consignment. The rate of excise duty for novel products shall be PLN 141,29 per kilogram and 31,41%. the weighted average retail selling price of smoking tobacco. In this case, it should be considered that the proposed taxation is very complicated and in no way falls within the common expectation of simple tax enforcement techniques. According to the government's calculation, the introduction of these two new taxes will increase the revenue of the state budget by about PLN 75 million per year. The project under investigation was subject to public consultation, although in a very short period of time, but it triggered quite a lively discussion, which resulted in a number of new proposals and solutions and had overall negative assessments. Entrepreneurs' representations mainly point to the introduction of new taxes without pre-recognizing and assessing the e-cigarette market. On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I request that the bill be submitted to the Public Finance Committee for further work.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u432	A motion to reject the draft law at first reading was put forward in the discussion. We will proceed to the vote on this proposal in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 13. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act and some other laws (Print No. 1931). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Minister Łukasz Piebiak to present the justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u433	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Today I have the honour to present to you a draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act and some other laws. The draft implements the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on a European Criminal Investigation Order. The aim of this Directive is to ensure an efficient and effective system of mutual assistance in criminal matters between the Member States of the European Union in the field of evidence activities. This objective is intended to be achieved by the draft implementing the Directive. Thanks to the regulations contained in the proposed law, the Polish court or body conducting the investigation in Poland will be able to apply to another Member State of the Union for proof in that country. Proposals may also be issued prior to the initiation of criminal proceedings for the purpose of conducting operational and exploratory activities. If the application is submitted by a body other than the court or prosecutor, it will have to be approved by the prosecutor. Similarly, the countries of the Union will be able to ask Poland to carry out evidence on the territory of the Republic of Poland. Requests for evidence may relate to any activity authorised under the national law of the requesting State, e.g. It is a question of: hearing, showing, searching, carrying out evidence from experts' opinions, intercepting telecommunications communications, reporting of bank accounts and transactions, ongoing monitoring of transactions in these accounts or secretly supervising shipments. At the same time, it will be possible to refuse the requested action in certain cases, e.g. where carrying out evidence threatens national security or violates human and citizen rights. It will also be possible to carry out another action if the requested action would be inadmissible from the legal point of view of the requested State. It should be stressed that the scope of cooperation under the directive and the law implementing it does not go beyond the existing instruments of cooperation within the Union. The Directive replaced trade with Member States under the 2000 EU Convention on Mutual Legal Assistance in Criminal Matters, as well as the 1959 European Council of Europe Convention on Legal Assistance in Criminal Matters, together with additional protocols to those Conventions. The directive and the law implementing it therefore do not introduce new cooperation mechanisms compared to the instruments currently in force. However, the procedure and procedure for implementing the application are changing. The amendments consist in setting deadlines for the implementation of proposals and clarifying the grounds for refusal. Short deadlines should contribute to speeding up the execution of applications and thus to a more effective conduct of criminal proceedings and a faster completion of criminal proceedings. On the other hand, clarification of the grounds for refusal will, on the one hand, facilitate the enforcement of requests under the law of the requested State and, on the other hand, facilitate the refusal to comply with those requests which would jeopardise the interests of the State. In view of the above, I propose that the Sejm adopt a law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u439	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The government bill amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act and some other laws are designed to allow Polish courts, prosecutors and police to issue a European investigation order. The introduction of these regulations will allow for better and more effective cooperation with the countries of the European Union in prosecuting criminals. The draft law presented by the government implements an EU directive in Poland under which the European Criminal Investigation Order is used. The EU Directive introduces an effective mechanism relating to mutual legal assistance in criminal matters between the Member States of the European Union as regards evidence by means of the possibility of issuing a European Investigation Order. The proposed law adapts Polish law to EU rules relating to the European Criminal Investigation Order. It should be stressed that the European Investigation Order will replace the existing proceedings on the protection of evidence only in relations with those States which are bound by the Directive. Thanks to the legal regulations used in the Directive, the Polish court or body conducting the investigation will be able to request another EU country to provide evidence in that country. Thanks to the new regulations, the Polish court, prosecutor's office or other investigating authority (e.g. The police, the Border Guard, the Customs-Scarb Service) will have the right to request any EU Member State to execute an investigation order. It should be stressed that the issuing of this order by a law enforcement authority other than the prosecutor will require the approval of the prosecutor. The issuing of a European Investigation Order may involve the carrying out of various types of evidence, i.e. activities such as: hearing, examining, familiarising with documents, checking and recording telephone calls, providing information on bank accounts and transactions in those accounts. At the same time, EU countries will be able to ask Poland to carry out evidence on our country's territory. However, there will be a possibility of another action if the requested action would be inadmissible from the point of view of the internal law of the requested State. The Government's draft amendment also allows the possibility of issuing an investigation order prior to the formal initiation of the investigation procedure, at the examination stage and on the basis of separate provisions (e.g. the Police Act, the Internal Security Agency and the Intelligence Agency Act). This will enable the investigating authority to obtain the evidence or information necessary to decide whether or not to initiate the investigation. The legislation so far allowed cross-border applications for legal aid, but the rules were very fragmented and complicated. As a result, they were reluctant to use them in practice. The European Investigation Order provides for a specific timeframe for carrying out the requested action and strictly limits the possibility of refusal. Greater communication and faster evidence-taking will positively increase the effectiveness of criminal proceedings conducted in Poland. While we are aware of the need for legal changes in this area, the proposals for changes presented in the government project are positive.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u442	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party our position on the government draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act and some other laws, print No. 1931. The project implements into the Polish legal order Directive 2014/41/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014 on a European Criminal Investigation Order. The aim of the project is to ensure an efficient and effective system of mutual legal assistance in criminal matters between EU Member States in the field of evidence (inquiry) by introducing the possibility of issuing a European Investigation Order (END). The European Investigation Order established by Directive 2014/41/EU is a mechanism for the transmission of requests by authorities from one country to carry out evidence activities such as, for example, the witness hearing, the demonstration or trial experiment in the other country and the feedback from the authorities of the other country on the results of these activities. It does not provide for a catalogue of investigative activities that may be carried out on the basis of it. Instead, it allows EU Member State authorities to apply for any investigative action authorised under their national law. The restriction is merely an enumerative of the grounds for refusing to execute the order and the possibility of carrying out another similar action where the requested action would be inadmissible under the law of the requested State. However, the rule is to recognise and enforce such an order in accordance with the principle of mutual trust between EU Member States. The Directive applies inter alia. to temporarily release persons who are deprived of freedom. Persons deprived of liberty covered by the order are to be issued for the purpose of testifying as a witness or taking part in other proceedings. They will therefore not be suspects and accused, issued for the purpose of prosecuting or serving penalties, which can only be transferred on the basis of a European arrest warrant. It is worth noting that the Directive applies to the means of proof used exclusively in criminal matters and therefore does not cover similar measures applied in civil or administrative proceedings. At the same time, because of the costs of the application of the order, it is not envisaged that an order may be issued in criminal proceedings. Unfortunately, Poland failed to implement the European Investigation Order within the required time limit, i.e. until 22 May 2016 It is good that today the High Chamber will finally address this necessary, improving the functioning of justice in Poland and in other EU countries. The adoption of the EU Directive in this area will facilitate the transmission of requests for evidence and the feedback of the results. Thanks to the regulations contained in the Directive, the Polish court or body conducting the investigation will be able to request another EU country to carry out evidence in that country. Similarly, EU countries will be able to apply to Poland for proof on the territory of the Republic of Poland.

The project does not generate additional costs as this concerns better use of funds already earmarked for this purpose, as enshrined in the 2015 Act. According to the aid scheme, these costs are expected to be around 153 million, including 53 million for restructuring public debt. As I said, the Modern Club will vote in favour of transferring the project to committee work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u458	Dear Marshal! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I request that the Government draft law amending the Restructuring Law and the Code of Administrative Procedure, print no. 1932, be continued.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u459	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the draft law amending the Restructuring Law and the Code of Administrative Procedure Act, print No. 1932. The amendment aims to enable public support to be provided to the entities concerned by the restructuring procedure without prejudice to Union law and without the need to notify the European Commission of support which is authorised by Union law. The amendments proposed in this project address the following issues. First, they relate to the adaptation of the provisions of the Law of 15 May 2015. Restructuring law to the conditions laid down by the European Commission, which will allow the granting of public aid for the restructuring of small and medium-sized enterprises without individual notification to the European Commission. This will significantly improve the provision of this assistance by eliminating the need for every expectation of the European Commission's decision (in practice the average waiting time for the EC to issue a decision on this matter is around 15 months). Second, they concern the restoration of a statutory authorisation to issue implementing rules similar to those contained in the Decree of 20 March 2015 of the Minister of the Treasury, which was repealed. This will enable the adoption of a new regulation, which together with this project will enable the launch of the aid scheme and will provide a complete basis for the granting of rescue and restructuring aid to firms. Thirdly, they concern amendments to the Article. 189 c.p.a. to the extent that the administrative penalties provided for in this Regulation may be granted as public restructuring aid and to the extent that they could be more closely regulated by the Council of Ministers' Implementing Regulation. Given that the proposed changes will shorten and simplify the procedure for granting public aid to small and medium-sized enterprises and that the aid will restore the company's well-being, enabling it to operate on the market, the Platform is in favour of directing the project to further legislative work.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-08-sejm-51-1.u460	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position towards the Government's draft law amending the Restructuring Law and the Code of Administrative Procedure Act. The purpose of the proposed law is to adapt the provisions to the conditions set out by the European Commission in the notification procedure of the aid scheme providing for rescue and restructuring aid to small and medium-sized enterprises. By amending the Act, it will be possible to grant public aid for restructuring without individual notification to the European Commission. The aim is to eliminate the need for every expectation of a decision by the European Commission, which is expected on average to be around 15 months. The introduction of changes will therefore have a real impact on improving the provision of assistance. Using the amendments introduced, it will be possible to achieve the expected effects of the aid scheme mentioned above. In addition, the proposed amendment aims to create statutory powers for the issuing of implementing rules similar to those contained in the previous Regulation which has expired. The changes that have been made in my assessment will improve the situation of companies waiting for support to come out of a difficult situation. I believe that the proposed change will have a positive impact on a faster return to viability and reduce the risk of business failure. The revised amendment introduces clear and transparent procedures for obtaining public aid for entrepreneurs. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceedings over the bill.
